# Human baseline

In this document an expert answers 21 FactoryBench items, one per active question template, with access to technical documentation, writing out the reasoning for each. This gives a human reference point for the model numbers and direct evidence on whether the questions are solvable from the context the benchmark supplies.

| | |
|---|---|
| Items | 21 (4 L1 questions, 10 L2 questions, 5 L3 questions, 2 L4 questions) |
| Source | `FactoryBench/FactoryBench`, (https://huggingface.co/datasets/FactoryBench/FactoryBench) |
| Selection | `seed=42` per template |

---

```text
Question: The robot is performing a manipulation task. We want to isolate the descent to the bin in the robot's time series. Assuming a fixed window length of 13 timesteps, at which timestamp should the window begin? Answer only with an integer or decimal number, nothing else.
```

</details>

**Answer:** 1067

*Why:* The window should begin in t=1067, as all jount velocities drop near zero (fs0=-1.11, fs1=0.14, fs2=0.81, fs3=-0.9, fs4=0.03, fs5=-0.8). This data indicates the robot has completed its horizontal approach and is momentarily at rest over the bin. After this timestamp, the base fp0 seems to be static, but the shoulder fs1, elbow fs2 and wrist fs3 had an increase in velocity, corresponding to a pure downward vertical translation (t=1166: fp0=107.22, fp1=-73.32, fp2=86.76, fp3=-102.08, fp4=-89.51, fp5=405.17, fs0=-1.51, fs1=3.5, fs2=13.95, fs3=-17.52, fs4=-0.02, fs5=-1.15, sp0=107.23, sp1=-73.32, sp2=86.8, sp3=-102.12, sp4=-89.51, sp5=405.17).

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`1067`

Acceptance bounds: `{"min": 767, "max": 1358}`

</details>

> What changed between the two instances of robotic time series data?  Answer only with a 4 letter string using F and T to indicate your answers (ie. TFFT to indicate True, False, False, True). Do not output anything else.

- **B.** The two robots have different anomalous states. Note: this means either exactly one of them is anomalous, or they both are, but have different anomalies.
- **C.** The two robots are performing different tasks.
- **D.** The two robots are performing the same task, but at different phases.

```text
Question: What changed between the two instances of robotic time series data?  Answer only with a 4 letter string using F and T to indicate your answers (ie. TFFT to indicate True, False, False, True). Do not output anything else.
Here are the options:
A. Those come from different robots.
B. The two robots have different anomalous states. Note: this means either exactly one of them is anomalous, or they both are, but have different anomalies.
C. The two robots are performing different tasks.
D. The two robots are performing the same task, but at different phases.
```

</details>

**Answer:** `FFTF`

**Why:**
- **A** is false as nothing in either stream requires a second machine. Series_b's torques all sit inside UR3e ratings, peaking at ett1=12.48 Nm against 56 Nm on the shoulder and never above 0.3 Nm on any wrist joint against 12 Nm there, and its pose (fp=0.2, -1, 1.41, -0.41, 2.55, -1.55) is simply the all-zeros pose, where that constant ett1=-12.48 Nm is the gravity hold expected of a 3 kg-class cobot with the arm horizontal. Series_a carries no torque channel to contradict any of it and satisfies fp1+fp2+fp3 = -87.32+127.43-130.11 = -90.0 with fp4=-90.58, the wrist-down configuration of the same class of arm. Item 12 is where the same test does force two machines, its series_a exceeding the 12 Nm wrist rating by 2.2x.
- **B** is false as neither stream is faulted. Series_a tracks its setpoint to within 0.05° end to end (t=4432: fp2=102.06 vs sp2=102.04), and series_b never leaves fs=0 while its contact-force estimate simply relaxes from ecf2=22.97 N to ≈0 N by t=1878.
- **C** is the only statement I mark true as series_a seems like it runs a pick-and-place cycle with 2.9 s of idle, then the base held at fp0=48.25 while shoulder, elbow and wrist 1 drive a pure vertical descent (t=3324: fs1=-20.96, fs2=-28.48, fs3=49.46), then a coordinated retract in which every joint moves (t=4736: fp0=45.56, fp5=342.87, fs5=-37.77), whereas series_b holds a single pose for 3 s and then creeps one wrist joint 1.5° over 1.7 s (fp4 2.55 → 1.05, peak fs4=-1.57), a fine in-place alignment rather than another phase of the same transport, which is what makes **D** false.


<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`FFTF`

</details>

```text
Question: What robot does this sensor data originate from? Answer only with the letter of the correct option (ie. A), nothing else.
Here are the options:
A. Universal Robots UR3e
B. KUKA KR 10 R1100-2
C. Agile Robots Yu 5 Industrial
```

</details>

**Answer:** `B`

**Why:** During the 1.5 seconds in which the arm is at static rest (fp = 82.66, -35.2, 74.86, -5.69, 44.43, 122.1 from t=0 to t=1617), the shoulder joint ett_1 must exert a force of between -95 and -102 Nm. Once the arm begins a slow dragging motion, this value stabilises between –134 and –148 Nm (t=2322: ett_1=-148.44). These values are physically incompatible with a Universal Robots UR3e (https://www.universal-robots.com/products/ur3e/), it has a nominal limit of 56 Nm on its main joints (base, shoulder, elbow). The recorded 148 Nm exceeds the physical limit of that machine. The Agile Robots Yu 5 is a 5 kg payload cobot whose proximal motors operate in the ~150 Nm range; recording 148 Nm would mean that the robot is operating at over 90% of its rated capacity simply to perform a slow movement in the middle of its workspace. As detailed in the KUKA KR 10 R1100-2 Technical Manual (https://es.scribd.com/document/754657260/kr10-r1100-2pdf#:~:text=Workspace%20graphic), this model is a has 10 kg payload and 1101 mm reach in an arm mass of approximately 54 kg. Sustaining ~100 Nm of static gravity load just to hold its own weight and plateauing at ~140 Nm during slow spatial movements, are completely routine and nominal values given the structural inertia of this specific KUKA hardware. In the industrial brochure of Yu 5 (https://www.agile-robots.com/media/files/products/Yu/Agile-Robots_Yu_5_Industrial_Brochure.pdf), this is a 5 kg payload collaborative robot. While its proximal motors do operate in the ~150 Nm maximum peak range, recording a sustained 148 Nm would mean that the robot is operating at over 90% of its rated peak capacity simply to perform a slow movement in the middle of its workspace.


<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`B`

</details>

```text
Question: Given the sensor stream below, what is the expected value of the position of joint 4 at T+7ms? Answer only with an integer or decimal number, nothing else.
```

</details>

**Answer:** -90.91

**Why:** Joint 4 is completely stationary (fs4 = 0, sp4 = -90.90 throughout). The measured position fp4 simply dithers between -90.90 and -90.91 due to 0.01° sensor quantization noise. Because the 7 ms prediction horizon is just a fraction of the ~100 ms sample period, mechanical dynamics cannot develop in that timeframe. Therefore, the current value will persist. -90.91 is the best estimate as it is the most frequent reading.

**Answer:** `BDAC`



**Why:** **B** should start at t=11391 as it has the values fs1=-0.03, fs2=-0.09, fs3=0.04 with fp1=-48.43, fp2=69.70, **D** at t=11998 with values fs1=-0.01, fs2=-0.01, fs3=-0.01 with fp1=-48.46, **A** at t=12601 with two identical rows, then fp1=-48.46 and fp3=-111.48, then fp1=-48.45 and fp3=-111.47 and then two more identical rows, and **C** at t=13207 with values fp5=323.21 on five consecutive rows, dropping to 323.20 on the sixth. The fault here is a TCP frame misconfiguration, a static parametric error, so there is no force or current transient in the option rows to order them by: every window shows the same parked pose (fp = 26.37, -48.45, 69.67, -111.47, -90.77, 323.2), the same joint temperatures (jt = 32.9 … 40.62) and tm=0. The four windows are consecutive slices of the settling tail of the context, where the residual joint speeds decay monotonically (fs1: -0.07 at t=11190 → -0.21 → -0.03 → -0.01 → 0 by t=12299). Once B and D are pinned onto that decay, the two all-zero-speed windows A and C are fixed as the next two slices by their duplication and fp5 patterns. The consistency check is that B → D → A → C is contiguous at 6 samples × ~101 ms per window with no gap, which is what tells me the match is right rather than coincidental.

`BDAC`

</details>

**Answer:** FFFF

**Why:** The stream carries only `feedback_pos`, `feedback_speed` and `setpoint_pos` so there is no contact-force channel (option A), no robot-current channel (B) and no joint-temperature channel (D), so none of those three claims can be asserted and I mark them false. **C** is computable and it is false, as the position error |fp − sp| peaks at 0.08° in the fastest part of the window (t=412: fp2=77.06 vs sp2=77.14, fp3=-62.38 vs sp3=-62.46) and falls to 0.02° by the end (t=3819: fp0=51.22 vs sp0=51.20) as the joint speeds decay from fs2=57.58 to fs2=-5.82.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`FFFF`

</details>

- **A.** Following the event, joint speeds stay close to baseline (within ±9% of pre-event values).
- **B.** Following the event, robot current increases markedly (>=21% above pre-event mean).
- **C.** Following the event, command and measured TCP motion remain aligned (TCP tracking error increase <=7%).
- **D.** Following the event, at least one joint speed drops sharply (>=25% below pre-event baseline).

```text
Question: The sensor stream below is from a robot exhibiting a TCP frame misconfiguration. Select all statements that apply in the options provided. Answer only with a 4 letter string using F and T to indicate your answers (ie. TFFT to indicate True, False, False, True). Do not output anything else.
Here are the options:
A. Following the event, joint speeds stay close to baseline (within ±9% of pre-event values).
B. Following the event, robot current increases markedly (>=21% above pre-event mean).
C. Following the event, command and measured TCP motion remain aligned (TCP tracking error increase <=7%).
D. Following the event, at least one joint speed drops sharply (>=25% below pre-event baseline).
```

</details>

**Answer:** `FFTT` 

**Why:** **A** is false and **D** true from the speed channels alone as joint speeds are nowhere near a ±9 % band, they sweep from 0 to fs1=-72.54 and fs5=-111.38 (t=706 of the second move) and back to 0 twice over, and at least one joint drops far more than 25 % below its pre-event level, fs0 goes from -54.26 at t=5445 to -3.19 at t=9979 and then to 0. **C** is true as command and measurement stay locked together, |fp − sp| never exceeds 0.07° anywhere in the 152 rows (t=5142: fp0=66.45 vs sp0=66.38; t=18051: fp0=37.87 vs sp0=37.94) and that residual is pure velocity lag. **B** is false as there is no robot-current signal in the stream.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`FFTT`

</details>

**Answer:** 134.2



**Why:** It looks like the arm is fully parked at the end of the record. From t = 8062 onwards, fp2 reads between 134.18 and 134.20, fs2 sits between 0.00 and 0.06, and sp2 remains at 134.20 for every sample up to t = 9778. 605 ms later, the best estimate is that the value remains the same. The collision appears as a violent excursion between t = 4237 and t = 7761, with fs0 reaching -181.87 and fs1 reaching -138.30 at t = 7761. Then, within two samples, everything collapses, with fp1 swinging out to -145.95. By t = 7962, the speeds have already decreased to fs0 = 40.74, and by t = 8062, all six are at 0, with fp2 = 134.23 against sp2 = 134.20. The final 1.7 seconds are a dead hold with a constant setpoint. 605 ms is comfortably within a hold that the controller has already maintained for longer, so persistence is the correct model rather than extrapolating the pre-stop dynamics.

```text
Question: The sensor stream below is from a robot exhibiting a collision with a cardboard object. What are the expected values of joint positions at T+981ms? Answer only with a list of 6 numbers (integer or decimal) formatted as a JSON array, ie. [10.2,9,2.1,1,7,0.21]. Do not return anything else.
```

</details>

**Answer:** [20.67,-66.29,62.49,2.54,21.18,90.66]



**Why:** The trajectory in this record finishes at t=2748, where fp2 tops out at 62.50 with fs2 dropping to 0.36, every sample from t=2849 onward is a hold. The only thing that happens afterwards is one outlier frame at t=4912 (fp = 20.91, -67.06, 63.24, 2.58, 21.42, 91.72 — 0.24 to 1.06° off the hold on all six joints at once, with sp shifted by the same amount and fs still ≈0), which is a spliced or mis-joined frame rather than motion, so I exclude it from the estimate. Choosing sp over the final fp row matters on joint 5, the last feedback value is 90.65 while the setpoint is 90.66, and on a joint this quiet the setpoint is the better predictor of where the arm actually is a second later. The pose sp = (20.67, -66.29, 62.49, 2.54, 21.18, 90.66) is identical in every row from t=4981 to t=5793, all six speeds stay within ±0.4 deg/s of zero over the same span, and fp jitters ±0.02° around that setpoint, so 981 ms ahead the arm is still sitting on it.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`[20.668171,-66.29869,62.472552,2.537959,21.179646,90.663381]`

Acceptance bounds: `{"signal": "feedback_pos", "std": [0.013178, 0.85298, 5.711012, 4.874785, 0.031276, 0.00909], "margin": [0.009883, 0.639735, 4.283259, 3.656089, 0.023457, 0.006818]}`

</details>

```text
Question: Knowing that the robot suffers from a collision with a rigid object in the given context time series, we want to isolate the descent to the object phase. Assuming a fixed window length of 12 timesteps, at which timestamp should the window begin? Answer only with an integer or decimal number, nothing else.
```

</details>

**Answer:** 1311


**Why:** Up to t=1311, the arm is sweeping around a lot (fp0 39.39 → 3.05 and fp5 313.55 → 253.08, with fs0 down to -50.09 and fs5 to -83.90), and at t=1311, all six velocities come together (fs0=-0.56, fs1=1.56, fs2=-0.46, fs3=-0.71, fs4=-0.08, fs5=-1.44). The approach is finished and the tool is resting above the object for a moment. From the next sample, the signature shows a downward movement instead of a continuation of the sweep. The base and the wrist 3 stop at 3.07-3.10 and stay there. The rest of the record shows that fs0 and fs5 never exceed 0.23 magnitude. At the same time, shoulder, elbow and wrist 1 show the pattern of a straight-line drop (t=1716: fs1=17.42, fs2=30.06, fs3:-48.32; fp1 -80.64 → -72.81, fp2 98.13 → 109.56, fp3 -107.94 → -127.18). This phase ends at t=2119, so a 12-sample window opened at t=1311 covers the descent, the contact and the first samples of the hold that follows — which is what you want if you are isolating the approach to the object.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`1311`

Acceptance bounds: `{"min": 1009, "max": 1614}`

</details>

### 11 · L2 · MC single-select

Question ID: `45624f64-f16f-4b06-9741-562fbd613de4`

> Given the sensor data from a robot performing the task, determine what anomaly is present? Answer only with a letter indicating your answer (ie. A, B, C or D). Do not output anything else.

- **A.** The peg approaches the hole at an angular or lateral offset, causing the tip to jam against the hole rim rather than enter cleanly, producing elevated contact forces and position tracking errors.
- **B.** Voraus-AD dataset anomaly class 0. Semantic description pending curation.
- **C.** The vacuum gripper releases the can mid-trajectory at a random point during transport, causing an abrupt loss of payload load on the robot arm.
- **D.** An external force continuously pulls or pushes the robot arm during motion, such as a tethered cable, hanging weight, or operator interference, causing persistent torque anomalies.

```text
Question: Given the sensor data from a robot performing the task, determine what anomaly is present? Answer only with a letter indicating your answer (ie. A, B, C or D). Do not output anything else.
Here are the options:
A. The peg approaches the hole at an angular or lateral offset, causing the tip to jam against the hole rim rather than enter cleanly, producing elevated contact forces and position tracking errors.
B. Voraus-AD dataset anomaly class 0. Semantic description pending curation.
C. The vacuum gripper releases the can mid-trajectory at a random point during transport, causing an abrupt loss of payload load on the robot arm.
D. An external force continuously pulls or pushes the robot arm during motion, such as a tethered cable, hanging weight, or operator interference, causing persistent torque anomalies.
```

</details>

**Answer:** D



**Why:** The record shows a sustained torque offset on the joints with no matching motion or tracking error. Between t=2919 and t=3018, the arm is motionless at fixed points (-17.59, -30.38, 63.47, 3.79, 50.21, 77.62), and from t=3018 et1 increases through -126.87, -142.82, -153.37, -162.86, and on to -178.16 (t=3925), staying between -168 and -178 Nm for the final 2.2 s, while et2 goes from -18 to -61 Nm. During this interval, the arm moves only a few degrees. An external force pulls on the arm.. Option C doesn't make sense because dropping a payload would suddenly reduce the force on the shoulder, but the data shows the torque (ett1) getting almost twice as big, which suggests that there's more weight added rather than lost. Option A is ruled out by the system's perfect tracking. If a peg got jammed, it would physically stop the arm and cause a big difference between the commanded position (SP) and the actual position (FP). But the tracking error here stays the same size (about 0.05°) throughout the sustained torque plateau. Finally, Option B must be rejected because it is an undefined placeholder ("Semantic description pending curation") that offers no physical anomaly signature to evaluate against the telemetry.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`D`

</details>

---

### 12 · L2 · MC multi-select

Question ID: `180f8bc9-d63e-494b-ae21-4db56980e55d`

> You are provided with two sensor streams originating from robots accomplishing tasks. What differences between the two given instances of robotic time series data (if any) do you notice? Answer only with a 4 letter string using F and T to indicate your answers (ie. TFFT to indicate True, False, False, True). Do not output anything else.

- **A.** Those come from different robots.
- **B.** The two robots have different anomalous states. Note: this means either exactly one of them is anomalous, or they both are, but have different anomalies.
- **C.** The two robots are performing different tasks.
- **D.** The two robots are performing the same task, but at different phases.

```text
Question: You are provided with two sensor streams originating from robots accomplishing tasks. What differences between the two given instances of robotic time series data (if any) do you notice? Answer only with a 4 letter string using F and T to indicate your answers (ie. TFFT to indicate True, False, False, True). Do not output anything else.
Here are the options:
A. Those come from different robots.
B. The two robots have different anomalous states. Note: this means either exactly one of them is anomalous, or they both are, but have different anomalies.
C. The two robots are performing different tasks.
D. The two robots are performing the same task, but at different phases.
```

</details>

**Answer:** `TTTF`

**Why:**
- **A** is true as series_a peaks at ett3=26.22 Nm on wrist 1 (t=2490) and ett4=17.41 Nm on wrist 2 (t=5278), which is 2.2 and 1.5 times the 12 Nm a UR3e is rated for on each of its three wrist joints, so series_a cannot come from a UR3e whatever its other channels look like, whereas series_b is a UR3e throughout, with wrist 2 pinned at -89.49° for the whole record, fp1+fp2+fp3 = -85.31+99.21-103.05 = -89.15 holding the tool vertical, and wrist 3 tracking the base at a fixed 0.28° offset.
- **B** is true and the call rests on torque per degree of travel rather than on torque magnitude. At t=2291 to t=2490 the six torques of series_a swing from about 0 to ett2=+47.28, ett1=-37.64 and ett3=+26.22 Nm while the joints move 0.05° in total and no speed exceeds 0.9 deg/s, and the same recurs at t=4780 to t=5278, where ett0 goes from -7.16 to -29.62 and ett4 from -4.83 to -17.41 for 0.64° of travel on joint 0. Tens of newton metres delivered into almost no displacement is a blocked or externally loaded joint, whereas series_b is a textbook ramp out of idle with tracking error at or below 0.05° throughout (t=4222: fp0=53.93 against sp0=53.97), so exactly one of the two streams is faulted.
- **C** is true and **D** therefore false, as series_a spends the whole record on micro adjustments inside a 1° envelope whereas series_b holds one pose for 3.5 s and then launches a fast coordinated sweep of base and wrist (t=4222: fp0=53.93, fs0=20.10, fs5=20.15), two different tasks rather than two phases of one.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`TTTF`

</details>

### 13 · L2 · MC single-select

Question ID: `427da1e6-77dc-4f46-9bed-5b7894161a4f`

> Knowing that the robot suffers from an external arm disturbance in the given context time series, what robot does this sensor data originate from? Answer only with the letter of the correct option (ie. A), nothing else.

- **A.** Agile Robots Yu 5 Industrial
- **B.** Universal Robots UR3e
- **C.** KUKA KR 10 R1100-2

<details>

```text
Question: Knowing that the robot suffers from an external arm disturbance in the given context time series, what robot does this sensor data originate from? Answer only with the letter of the correct option (ie. A), nothing else.
Here are the options:
A. Agile Robots Yu 5 Industrial
B. Universal Robots UR3e
C. KUKA KR 10 R1100-2
```

</details>

**Answer:** `B`

**Why:** wrist 2 sits at -89.07 or -89.08° for all 41 samples, the sum over shoulder, elbow and wrist 1 is -81.58 + 107.92 - 115.50 = -89.16, which is about -90° and holds the tool vertical, and wrist 3 tracks the base at a constant offset in every single row (t=0: fp0=60.95 against fp5=60.66, t=1454: 54.93 against 54.57, t=4065: 48.42 against 48.08, always fp5 equal to fp0 minus 0.33, base and wrist 3 rotating together to keep tool yaw fixed while the base sweeps). That is the same UR3e cell and program family as the other UR3e traces in this set. The other two options are excluded on channel set and on scale. This stream publishes `feedback_pos`, `feedback_speed` and `setpoint_pos` and nothing else, whereas the KUKA trace in this benchmark, publishes axis torques with no joint speed channel at all and sits in a completely different pose family (fp3=-5.69, fp4=+44.43), so C is out. The Agile Robots streams always carry `effort_target_torque` and often `est_contact_force`, and they operate within 3° of zero, whereas this record has neither and runs at 48, -81, 108 and -115°, so A is out. Everything else fits a UR3e, as peak speeds of 25 deg/s against a limit of 180 deg/s, sampling near 100 ms in the RTDE style, tracking error at or below 0.08° (t=1557: fp0=53.05 against sp0=52.97), and the stated external arm disturbance leaving no mark on any of the three published channels, which is itself consistent because a disturbance shows up in torque and this stream carries no torque.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`B`

</details>

**Answer:** `ACBD`

**Why:** **A** is the immediate continuation. Its first row reproduces the last context row exactly, fp = (107.21, -79.69, 104.93, -115.60, -90.51, 306.49) with fs = (0.51, 12.34, 20.82, -32.82, 0.06, 0) at t=4732, and across its six rows the descent finishes and the arm settles at fp1=-74.12, with the TCP force on Z climbing to tf2=33.01 and 32.90 on the last two rows, which is the impact. Every other option sits at that settled pose with fs=0, so their order follows the relaxation of the joint current and torque offsets. A ends at co1=-0.40 and co4=-0.09. **C** starts at co1=-0.52 and co4=-0.02 and climbs to co1=-1.52, co2=-1.30, co3=-0.60, co4=-0.09. **B** picks up at co1=-1.52, co2=-1.29, co3=-0.55, co4=-0.14 and drifts to co2=-1.22, co3=-0.51, co4=-0.15. **D** is flat at exactly those final values, co = (0.06, -1.52, -1.22, -0.51, -0.15, -0.02) in all seven rows.

A runs tp = 6, 6, 6, 6, 7, 7, 7 and so crosses a waypoint, B and C are entirely at tp = 7, and D ends at tp = 8, which puts A first and D last. Second the motion time field, which decays through A (tm = 0.61, 0.84, 0.55, 0.24, 0.24, 0, 0) and reads 0 in every row of B, C and D, exactly what you expect if A is the only window that still contains motion. The TCP force on Z tells the same story physically, peaking at +33 N in A, still decaying from +18.91 N in C's first row, and settled to a steady value near -13 N in B and D. No positional information can order these segments, since fp is identical to 0.01° across all four, so the whole item hinges on the auxiliary control channels.

- **C.** Following the event, joint speeds stay close to baseline (within ±12% of pre-event values).

```text
Question: Given the counterfactual scenario where a collision hanging cable occurs at timestep 7766 ms, what would most likely happen next? Answer only with a 4 letter string using F and T to indicate your answers (ie. TFFT to indicate True, False, False, True). Do not output anything else.
Here are the options:
A. No significant effect: safety stays normal and aggregate error (mean of tracking, TCP-tracking, force, and robot-current deviation terms) increases by <=14%.
B. Following the event, contact force shows a significant spike (>=41% above baseline norm).
C. Following the event, joint speeds stay close to baseline (within ±12% of pre-event values).
D. Following the event, tracking error increases noticeably (>=31% above pre-event mean).
```

</details>

**Answer:** `TFFF`

**Why:** **A** is True as the event is placed at 7766 ms, but the stream ends at 6353 ms and the arm has been at a complete commanded standstill since t=3835, with all six speeds reading exactly 0, fpo frozen at (34.82, -45.81, 64.80, -109.15, -90.84, 337.84) and the TCP setpoint constant for 2.5 s. A hanging cable brushing a parked arm has no relative velocity and no dynamic path to excite, so there is no mechanism by which it moves the safety state or the aggregate error. I consider **B**, **C** and **D** false because nothing in the released channels supports them. There is no force channel that could show a 41 % spike, there is no `setpoint_pos` to compute a joint tracking error against, and the ±12 % band in option C is taken around a joint speed that was exactly zero before the event.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`TFFF`

</details>

- **A.** Following the event, joint speeds stay close to baseline (within ±10% of pre-event values).
- **B.** Following the event, temperatures rise across multiple joints (>=3 joints with slope >0.36% of baseline per horizon).
- **C.** Following the event, tracking error increases noticeably (>=29% above pre-event mean).
- **D.** Following the event, robot current increases markedly (>=18% above pre-event mean).

```text
Question: Given the counterfactual scenario where a collision hanging cable occurs at timestep 7764 ms, select all statements that would apply. Answer only with a 4 letter string using F and T to indicate your answers (ie. TFFT to indicate True, False, False, True). Do not output anything else.
Here are the options:
A. Following the event, joint speeds stay close to baseline (within ±10% of pre-event values).
B. Following the event, temperatures rise across multiple joints (>=3 joints with slope >0.36% of baseline per horizon).
C. Following the event, tracking error increases noticeably (>=29% above pre-event mean).
D. Following the event, robot current increases markedly (>=18% above pre-event mean).
```

</details>

**Answer:** `FFFF`

**Why:** marked TO-CHECK because none of the four statements can be established. The event is placed at 7764 ms and the record ends at 3233 ms, with the arm parked since t=2322, all six speeds at about 0 and the pose frozen at (-4.81, -88.31, 128.45, -130.11, -89.70, 171.93). **B** needs joint temperatures and **D** needs robot current, and neither channel exists in the prompt. **C** needs a tracking error, and the stream publishes `setpoint_tcp` only, with no measured TCP pose and no `setpoint_pos`, so there is nothing to difference. **A** is stated as a ±10 % band around a joint speed that was exactly zero before the event, which is degenerate: the arm either stays at 0, on the boundary, or the program resumes and blows straight past it, and this same cycle reaches 153 deg/s on wrist 3 at t=807, so staying close to baseline cannot be asserted either.

The physical reading is unremarkable and the same in every case. A slack cable contacting a stationary arm 4.5 s after it stopped, at a pose it has been holding without any excursion visible in the published channels, is not going to raise motor current, heat three joints or open a tracking gap, so even where a channel were available I would not expect B, C or D to fire. What makes the item unanswerable rather than merely easy is that three of the four thresholds reference signals the prompt does not carry, so a solver arrives at FFFF by elimination rather than by measurement, the right string for the wrong reason.

```text
Question: In the counterfactual scenario where a collision cardboard object occurs at timestep 7965 ms, what would be the expected value of the velocity of joint 0 at T+1008ms? Answer only with an integer or decimal number, nothing else.
```

**Answer:** 0

**Why:** the move is already decelerating and will be over well before the horizon. The segment that started at t=2920 peaked at fs0=54.70 (t=3828) and is now decaying, 53.14 at t=3928 and 46.60 at t=4130, and the joints that lead it have already collapsed, fs1 from -61.07 to -9.93 and fs2 from 88.60 to 12.59 across those same three samples, with fp1 and fp2 flattening out from -90.42 to -92.26 and from 105.56 to 107.75. One second later joint 0 is parked. The record gives a precedent for how long a settle takes on this machine. The first segment runs from t=1912 to t=2820 and is at zero within roughly 900 ms of its deceleration onset, with the ramp down occupying 400 to 500 ms once the distal joints start unwinding, which they have already done here. T+1008 ms therefore lands inside the hold and not in the ramp, so 0 deg/s is the estimate rather than a linear extrapolation of the 6.5 deg/s per sample that fs0 is currently shedding, which would give about -19 and is meaningless past the stop. The counterfactual collision at 7965 ms is 3.8 s beyond the end of the record and does not enter a forecast one second long.

```text
Question: In the counterfactual scenario where a collision cardboard object occurs at timestep 14017 ms, what would be the expected values of joint motor torques at T+87ms? **Answer:** `[23.4,-136.0,-21.7,-1.9,6.1,0.65]`
```

**Why:** the arm is in the middle of a steady sweep and the torques are on a flat plateau, so the estimate one sample ahead is the current plateau level. I take the mean of the last five samples, t=4410 to t=4809, over which ett0 runs from 19.52 to 26.19, ett1 from -137.74 to -133.72, ett2 from -23.85 to -19.93, ett3 from -2.47 to -0.92, ett4 from 5.25 to 6.82 and ett5 from 0.46 to 0.90. From t=3707 to t=4809 joint 0 advances by a nearly constant 1.8° per 100 ms sample, from 5.46 to 25.59°, which is about 18 deg/s, while joints 1 to 4 change slowly and monotonically, and over that whole span each torque channel varies only within its noise band, with no trend beyond a gentle unloading of the shoulder, ett1 from -143.4 to -133.7 Nm or roughly 1 Nm per 100 ms. At a horizon of 87 ms, less than one sample period, neither that trend nor the counterfactual matters. The collision is placed at 14017 ms, some 9.2 s past the end of the record, and 87 ms is far shorter than any impact response. Averaging rather than taking the last row is deliberate, because the scatter from sample to sample on ett0 is about 4 Nm, 19.52 at t=4510 against 26.19 at t=4410, larger than the drift over the horizon, so the plateau mean is the estimate with the lower variance. I also discount t=4323 as a spliced frame, since fp1, fp2, fp4 and fp5 all jump off the smooth ramp by 0.3 to 1.2° at that one sample and return.

```text
Question: Given the sensor stream below, does the machine show signs of anomalous behavior? If yes, identify the most likely root cause and describe the steps you would take to fix it.
```

</details>

**Answer:** No. The machine is operating normally and no remediation is required.

**Why:** the one large event in the record, |ett1| stepping from about 85 to about 147 Nm and |ett2| from about 15 to about 53 Nm between t=10520 and t=10925, lands exactly on the onset of a commanded motion. sp1 and sp4 begin to change at t=10621, sp1 from -44.51 to -44.53 and sp4 from 34.41 to 34.43, and fp follows them, so this is the feedforward and gravity load step of a joint that starts moving and extending rather than a disturbance. It also has the right shape for that, overshooting to -147.00 Nm at t=10925 and then relaxing onto a quiet plateau between -137 and -146 Nm, which is acceleration followed by cruise. Position tracking backs it up. The error is exactly 0.00° through the standstill of 3.3 s from t=7193 to t=10520, where fp equals sp to two decimals on all six joints, and it only reaches 0.09 to 0.12° once the joints are moving (t=11929: fp1=-48.87 against sp1=-48.96, fp4=39.36 against sp4=39.48), which is plain lag proportional to velocity. There is no protective stop, no discontinuity and no chatter on the plateau.

The reason this record reads as healthy rather than as the external load case in item 11 is the shape of the torque profile and not its size. Both records show a hold near -85 to -100 Nm on the shoulder followed by a step into the range -140 to -178 Nm as the arm extends. In item 11 the torque climbs monotonically for the whole move, from -126.87 to -178.16 Nm, and never comes back, which is what a persistent pull looks like, whereas here it peaks at -147.00 Nm within 300 ms and then settles several newton metres lower and stays flat, which is what inertia followed by a gravity cruise looks like. The other things I checked and found clean: the plateau has no oscillation or ratcheting, staying at -141 ± 5 Nm across fifteen samples, the setpoint is never reissued or discontinuous, and the tracking error scales with speed instead of accumulating.

One feature I would put on a watch list rather than fix. During the standstill, where the pose is identical from one sample to the next, the hold torque drifts monotonically, ett1 from -76.58 to -88.97 Nm and ett2 from -12.21 to -15.12 Nm, which is 16 % and 24 % over 3.3 s. Gravity torque is a function of pose alone, so a drift at a fixed pose is either thermal drift in the torque estimate derived from motor current or a slowly applied external load. It is small in absolute terms, it produces no tracking degradation, and one window of 4.7 s is not enough to separate the two, so the action is to trend the hold torque at this pose across cycles rather than to intervene now.

```text
Question: An engineer wants to increase the effectiveness and accuracy of this machine. Based on the sensor stream below, what operational changes or parameter adjustments could help achieve this? Do not justify your answer, simply indicate steps to take (or specify if nothing can be done).
```

</details>

**Answer:**

1. Run the payload identification again for the current tool, both mass and centre of gravity, and write the corrected values into the tool load and installation settings.
2. Run the gravity and joint friction model identification again afterwards, so the feedforward matches the actual tool.
3. Reduce commanded path speed and acceleration on the part of the cycle where accuracy matters, or raise the velocity feedforward gain.
4. Use exact stop, blend radius 0, at the taught points where accuracy matters.
5. Reset the force and torque estimate to zero with the corrected payload, then trend the static hold torque at a fixed pose across cycles to confirm the correction took.

**Why:** marked TO-CHECK because the family of fixes is derivable from the stream but the numeric correction is not. There is no payload, tool load or installation settings channel in the prompt, so no specific centre of gravity vector can be read off it. The ordering above puts the load identification first on the strength of three observations. First, a systematic following error proportional to velocity: fp trails sp by 0.38° on joint 0 and 0.78° on joint 5 while they are running (t=14105: fp0=77.58 against sp0=77.20, fp5=39.04 against sp5=39.82), falling to 0.01° as soon as the arm stops (t=18947: fp0=17.07 against sp0=17.06), so accuracy is limited by tracking lag and not by repeatability. Second, a wrist torque that depends on the direction of travel and decomposes into friction plus a bias independent of pose: ett5 holds between +6.5 and +7.0 Nm through the whole first segment while joint 5 winds from 39.04 to 92.59°, then flips to about -3.8 Nm for the second segment while it unwinds, which gives a Coulomb term near 5.2 Nm and a residual bias near +1.4 Nm that a correct load model should null. With a payload of a few kilograms that bias corresponds to a lateral offset of the centre of gravity of roughly 0.1 m, and that is the quantitative hint that the tool load is misconfigured rather than merely unidentified. Third, ett2 drifts 22.8 Nm, from -53.99 to -31.22, during the final hold from t=19048 to t=19551, where no joint moves more than 0.15°, so the load model at that pose has not settled. All three are consistent with a mismatch in payload mass and centre of gravity.

<details>
<summary>Expected answer</summary>

`The payload center of gravity is set to [100,0,120] but the correct value is [0,0,120]. Update the CoG offset in the installation settings to [0,0,120].`

Root cause: `payload_cog_misconfiguration`

</details>

---
